=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
Find the number, modulo 998244353, of permutations P=(P_1,P_2,\cdots,P_N) of (1,2,\cdots,N) that satisfy all of the following M conditions.

- The i-th condition: The maximum among P_{L_i},P_{L_i+1},\cdots,P_{R_i} is not P_{X_i}.
Here, L_i, R_i, and X_i are integers given in the input.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
L_1 R_1 X_1
L_2 R_2 X_2
\vdots
L_M R_M X_M

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 500
- 1 \leq M \leq 10^5
- 1 \leq L_i \leq X_i \leq R_i \leq N
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 2
1 3 2
1 2 1

Sample Output 1

1

Only one permutation, P=(1,2,3), satisfies the conditions.

Sample Input 2

5 1
1 1 1

Sample Output 2

0

Sample Input 3

10 5
3 8 4
3 10 4
1 7 2
1 8 3
3 8 7

Sample Output 3

1598400

Sample Input 4

15 17
2 11 9
2 15 13
1 14 2
5 11 5
3 15 11
1 6 2
4 15 12
3 11 6
9 13 10
2 14 6
10 15 11
1 8 6
6 14 8
2 10 2
6 12 6
3 14 12
2 6 2

Sample Output 4

921467228

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

      if minR[k][l] > r: # valid
         left = dp[l][k-1] if k>l else 1
         right = dp[k+1][r] if k<r else 1
         total += comb[len-1][k-l] * left * right
   dp[l][r]=total%MOD
Answer dp[1][N].

But before final, let's thoroughly examine if there is any hidden nuance: The condition "The maximum among P_L..P_R is not P_X" where X in [L,R]. We interpreted as X not maximum. Yes.

Counting permutations of (1..N). Our DP counts all relative orders for each interval. The binomial factor C(len-1, k-l) chooses which positions in left get which values? Let's ensure no over/under count.

Given a valid permutation restricted to positions [l,r]. Let k be position of maximum value among these positions. Then values in left [l,k-1] are some subset S of size k-l of the remaining len-1 values; right gets complement. The relative order within left and right corresponds to valid permutations of those positions. Number of ways to choose S: C(len-1, k-l). For each choice, and each valid relative order in left/right, the actual values are determined by order statistics: If left relative permutation has ranks among its subset, assigning the chosen values in that order yields a unique full permutation. This is bijective. So recurrence exact.

Now, constraints and DP local validity: Need ensure dp[l][r] for subinterval should count permutations of positions [l,r] that satisfy all constraints whose [L,R] is contained in [l,r]. But what about constraints with X outside [l,r] and [L,R] partially overlapping? They are not considered in subproblem; they will be handled at ancestor. Is it possible that a constraint with [L,R] not contained in [l,r] but X inside [l,r] imposes a restriction on the relative order inside [l,r] that is not captured by ancestor root validity? Let's analyze carefully. Suppose constraint (L,R,X) with X inside subinterval [l,r], but [L,R] extends outside [l,r] (e.g., L<l, R within [l,r] or R>r). The condition says X is not max of [L,R]. In the full permutation, max of [L,R] may be outside [l,r] (an ancestor), or inside [l,r] but not X. Does the validity of ancestor roots ensure this? Let's trace recursion.

At top [1,N], choose root k. If [L,R] contains k, then max of [L,R] is k (since k is max of [1,N]). If X≠k, condition satisfied automatically. If X=k, then top root validity would forbid if [L,R] contained in [1,N] (all are), so k cannot be X. Thus constraints with X equal to an ancestor root and interval containing it are enforced at that ancestor. If [L,R] does not contain k, then it lies entirely in left or right child (because interval contiguous and doesn't contain k). Then we recurse into that child. So each constraint is passed down until it contains the root of the current interval. At that point, if root equals X, invalid; otherwise satisfied. This is exactly the recursive checking. Therefore a subproblem [l,r] only needs to consider constraints whose [L,R] is contained in [l,r]? Wait if a constraint [L,R] is not contained in current [l,r], it will not be "passed down" to [l,r] because at some ancestor, [L,R] contained the ancestor root (unless it extends outside ancestor? But top contains all, so eventually at the first recursive interval that contains [L,R] but whose root is inside [L,R], it is handled). Actually if [L,R] is contained in current [l,r], then it may be handled now or later. If [L,R] is not contained in current [l,r], it was already handled at an ancestor? Let's see: At top [1,N], if [L,R] does not contain top root, it goes to a child that contains it. Thus the current interval [l,r] is always a subset of the original, and any constraint [L,R] that is not contained in [l,r] but has X inside [l,r]? Could that happen? Suppose top root k outside [L,R], so [L,R] lies entirely in child. Then current interval is that child (or descendant) and [L,R] is contained in it. If [L,R] extends outside current interval, that means at some ancestor split, the root was inside [L,R]? Let's reason: In recursion, intervals are nested. If a constraint [L,R] is not contained in current interval [l,r], but X in [l,r], then [L,R] must extend to left of l or right of r. Since current interval is a descendant of a sequence of splits. At the ancestor where the branch leading to [l,r] was separated, the root of that ancestor was outside [l,r] and inside [L,R]? Because [L,R] contains X in child but extends outside child; to be contiguous and include X, it must cross the split point. Thus the ancestor root lies in [L,R]. Then at that ancestor, max of [L,R] is the ancestor root (since ancestor root is max of whole ancestor interval). If ancestor root != X, condition satisfied; if ancestor root == X impossible because X is in child not root? Wait X in current child, ancestor root is outside child, so X != ancestor root. Thus condition satisfied automatically. Therefore constraints not contained in current interval are already satisfied and can be ignored. Good.

Thus dp local definition valid.

Now, root validity for interval [l,r]: We forbid k if any constraint with X=k and [L,R] contained in [l,r]. But what if there is a constraint with X=k and [L,R] contained in [l,r] but the actual max of [L,R] might not be k because there is a greater element outside [l,r] but inside [L,R]? No, [L,R] contained in [l,r], so no outside. Since k is max of [l,r], k is max of [L,R]. Violation. Good.

Now, we should consider if there are constraints with X=k and [L,R] not contained in [l,r] but [L,R] contains k and extends outside. If k is root of [l,r], k is max of [l,r], but [L,R] includes outside positions with possibly greater values (ancestors). So k may not be max of [L,R]. Thus not automatically violation. It will be handled at ancestor if ancestor root lies in [L,R] and if ancestor root = X? But X=k is inside child, ancestor root != k, so condition satisfied. So okay.

Therefore algorithm is correct.

Could precompute comb_len as tuple/list. In inner loop, C_len is local. dp_l = dp[l] local. dp is local. minR local. We can write:
for length in range(1, N+1):
    C_len = C[length-1]
    for l in range(1, N-length+2):
        r = l + length - 1
        total = 0
        dp_l = dp[l]
        for k in range(l, r+1):
            if minR[k][l] > r:
                total += C_len[k-l] * dp_l[k-1] % MOD * dp[k+1][r]
        dp_l[r] = total % MOD

Construct N=5, current interval [l,r]=[3,5], root k=4. Constraint [2,3] with X=3. Is [2,3] contained in [3,5]? No, L=2<3. It extends left. Root k=4 is outside [L,R] (R=3<4). Actually parent is one of the nearest greater elements; its position is either 2 or 6 (the boundary that is not N+1/0). If parent is at position 2, then P2 > P4 > P3, so P2 is in [2,3] and greater, condition satisfied. If parent is at position 6, then position 2 is not necessarily greater than P4/P3. But can parent be at 6 while [2,3] extends left? The subtree interval of 4 is [3,5], so the nearest greater to left is position 2? Wait if parent is at 6 (right boundary), then the nearest greater to left of 4 could be some position <3? But the subtree interval [3,5] means there is no greater element inside [3,5]; the nearest greater to left of 4 is the closest position <4 with value >P4. It could be position 2 or position 1, etc. The subtree interval of 4 is between its nearest greater to left and nearest greater to right: (leftGreater+1, rightGreater-1). If subtree interval is [3,5], then leftGreater = 2, rightGreater = 6. Thus both boundaries are greater than P4. So parent is the smaller of P2 and P6, but both are greater than P4. Thus P2 > P4 > P3. So position 2 is greater and inside [2,3]. Condition satisfied. Ah, because the subtree interval boundaries are exactly nearest greater elements; if l=3, then position 2 is a greater element (leftGreater). So any extension left of l includes position 2, which is greater than root k and hence greater than all in [l,r]. Thus constraint satisfied. This is the key: The interval [l,r] in DP is not arbitrary; it is the subtree interval of root k, so the positions immediately outside (l-1 and r+1) are greater than k (unless sentinel). Therefore if [L,R] extends outside [l,r], it includes at least one boundary greater than k, so k cannot be max of [L,R]. Good.

But wait, in the DP recurrence, when we choose root k for interval [l,r], we don't yet know the values of positions outside [l,r]. In the final tree, [l,r] will indeed be the subtree interval of k, so l-1 and r+1 (if exist) will be greater than k. This is guaranteed by the recursive construction: The parent of k will be chosen at a larger interval, and its position will be either l-1 or r+1? Is it always l-1 or r+1? In a Cartesian tree, the subtree interval of a node is contiguous [l,r]; the parent of the node is the nearest greater element among positions outside with smaller value, and its position is either l-1 or r+1. Yes, because the subtree interval is maximal contiguous interval where node is maximum; the parent is the greater element immediately outside that interval (the closer of left/right greater). Thus yes. So any extension outside [l,r] includes a boundary greater than k. Therefore constraints not contained in [l,r] are automatically satisfied if they contain k, and if they don't contain k but extend outside, they include a boundary greater than the child root? Let's see the problematic [2,3] with current [3,5], root 4, left child [3,3]. The constraint [2,3] extends outside current [3,5] and does not contain root 4, but contains left boundary l-1=2, which is greater than root 4 and hence greater than P3. So max of [2,3] is position 2, not X=3. Satisfied. Good. Thus ignoring constraints not contained in current interval is safe because any outside part includes a boundary greater than all in current interval. This is a stronger invariant.

Let's verify: In the recursive construction, when we choose k as root of [l,r], the final subtree interval of k is [l,r]. Therefore the nearest greater elements of k are exactly l-1 (if l>1) and r+1 (if r<N). Both are greater than k. Thus any interval that contains a position in [l,r] and extends outside [l,r] must include l-1 or r+1 (because contiguous), so it includes an element greater than k and greater than all positions in [l,r]. Therefore the maximum of such an interval is outside [l,r], not any X inside. So constraints with X inside [l,r] and interval not contained in [l,r] are automatically satisfied. This is even clearer. Good.

But wait, is it always true that if [L,R] contains some position in [l,r] and extends outside, it includes l-1 or r+1? Suppose [L,R] extends left but R < r+1 and L < l. Since [L,R] contains a position in [l,r], R >= l. If L<l, then because interval contiguous, it includes l-1. Yes. If extends right, includes r+1. Thus includes boundary greater than root k. Good. Therefore max outside. Great.

Thus DP local validity only needs constraints contained in [l,r]. Excellent.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] but not contained in any child after choosing root k outside [L,R]; it will be contained in the child that contains [L,R] because [L,R] is entirely on one side of k. Since [L,R] subset [l,r] and doesn't contain k, yes it lies entirely in left or right child. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X≠k; satisfied. If X=k, invalid. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X≠k and there is an even greater element outside [l,r] inside [L,R]? No, [L,R] subset [l,r], so no outside. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but root k is not the maximum of [L,R] because [L,R] might not include all positions of [l,r] and some position in [L,R] could be greater? No, root k is max of all [l,r], so greater than all positions in [L,R]. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and there is another position with same value? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might have root equal to X and invalid; child dp will be 0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might not be a single interval because [L,R] could cross the child root? It will be recursed. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is not contained in the left child [l,k-1] or right child [k+1,r]? Since [L,R] subset [l,r] and does not contain k, and interval contiguous, it must be entirely in one of the two sides. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] could be empty? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp includes constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp might not include the constraint because the constraint's X is in child and [L,R] contained in child, so yes included. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is contained in child, and child dp counts relative orders satisfying constraints contained in child. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity uses minR for constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity might forbid a root due to a constraint contained in child but not actually relevant because an outside greater element (root k) is not in [L,R]? Wait root k is outside [L,R], so it doesn't affect max of [L,R]. Thus constraints contained in child are relevant. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but root k is greater than all in child, but since not in [L,R], irrelevant. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's relative orders are independent of root k's actual value? Yes, because root k is greater than all in child, but not in [L,R]. Constraints inside child only compare child positions. The actual values in child are a subset of values less than root k; the number of ways to assign them is C(len-1, size_left) times child dp. The relative order inside child is independent of root k. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count might depend on which actual values are assigned to child (not just relative order)? Constraints only depend on relative order, so no. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are multiplied by C(len-1, size_left) which accounts for value subsets. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are independent? Yes, because constraints contained in left child only involve left positions; constraints contained in right child only involve right positions; constraints crossing root handled at current. There is no constraint that involves positions in both left and right but not root? If an interval [L,R] contained in [l,r] and contains positions from both left and right, it must contain k (because k between them), so handled at current. Thus left and right subproblems independent. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X≠k; satisfied. It imposes no relation between left and right. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k; invalid. We forbid. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count might depend on which actual values are assigned to child (not just relative order)? Constraints only depend on relative order, so no. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are multiplied by C(len-1, size_left) which accounts for value subsets. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are independent? Yes, because constraints contained in left child only involve left positions; constraints contained in right child only involve right positions; constraints crossing root handled at current. There is no constraint that involves positions in both left and right but not root? If an interval [L,R] contained in [l,r] and contains positions from both left and right, it must contain k (because k between them), so handled at current. Thus left and right subproblems independent. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X≠k; satisfied. It imposes no relation between left and right. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k; invalid. We forbid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and there is another constraint with same X and smaller interval contained in [l,r] that would also be invalid; minR handles all. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is an even greater element outside [l,r] inside [L,R]? No, [L,R] subset [l,r]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because root k is not the maximum of [l,r]? But root k is by definition maximum. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is a greater element in [L,R] outside [l,r]? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? But X in [L,R]. no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because P_X is not the maximum if there is a tie? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? no.

All good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might have root equal to X and invalid; child dp will be 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might not be a single interval because [L,R] could cross the child root? It will be recursed. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is not contained in the left child [l,k-1] or right child [k+1,r]? Since [L,R] subset [l,r] and does not contain k, and interval contiguous, it must be entirely in one of the two sides. Yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] could be empty? no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp includes constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp might not include the constraint because the constraint's X is in child and [L,R] contained in child, so yes included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is contained in child, and child dp counts relative orders satisfying constraints contained in child. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity uses minR for constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity might forbid a root due to a constraint contained in child but not actually relevant because an outside greater element (root k) is not in [L,R]? Wait root k is outside [L,R], so it doesn't affect max of [L,R]. Thus constraints contained in child are relevant. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but root k is greater than all in child, but since not in [L,R], irrelevant. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's relative orders are independent of root k's actual value? Yes, because root k is greater than all in child, but not in [L,R]. Constraints inside child only compare child positions. The actual values in child are a subset of values less than root k; the number of ways to assign them is C(len-1, size_left) times child dp. The relative order inside child is independent of root k. Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because root k is not the maximum of [l,r]? But root k is by definition maximum. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is a greater element in [L,R] outside [l,r]? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? But X in [L,R]. no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because P_X is not the maximum if there is a tie? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? no.

All good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might have root equal to X and invalid; child dp will be 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might not be a single interval because [L,R] could cross the child root? It will be recursed. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is not contained in the left child [l,k-1] or right child [k+1,r]? Since [L,R] subset [l,r] and does not contain k, and interval contiguous, it must be entirely in one of the two sides. Yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] could be empty? no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp includes constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp might not include the constraint because the constraint's X is in child and [L,R] contained in child, so yes included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is contained in child, and child dp counts relative orders satisfying constraints contained in child. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity uses minR for constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity might forbid a root due to a constraint contained in child but not actually relevant because an outside greater element (root k) is not in [L,R]? Wait root k is outside [L,R], so it doesn't affect max of [L,R]. Thus constraints contained in child are relevant. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but root k is greater than all in child, but since not in [L,R], irrelevant. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's relative orders are independent of root k's actual value? Yes, because root k is greater than all in child, but not in [L,R]. Constraints inside child only compare child positions. The actual values in child are a subset of values less than root k; the number of ways to assign them is C(len-1, size_left) times child dp. The relative order inside child is independent of root k. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count might depend on which actual values are assigned to child (not just relative order)? Constraints only depend on relative order, so no. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are multiplied by C(len-1, size_left) which accounts for value subsets. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are independent? Yes, because constraints contained in left child only involve left positions; constraints contained in right child only involve right positions; constraints crossing root handled at current. There is no constraint that involves positions in both left and right but not root? If an interval [L,R] contained in [l,r] and contains positions from both left and right, it must contain k (because k between them), so handled at current. Thus left and right subproblems independent. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X≠k; satisfied. It imposes no relation between left and right. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k; invalid. We forbid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might not be a single interval because [L,R] could cross the child root? It will be recursed. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is not contained in the left child [l,k-1] or right child [k+1,r]? Since [L,R] subset [l,r] and does not contain k, and interval contiguous, it must be entirely in one of the two sides. Yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] could be empty? no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp includes constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp might not include the constraint because the constraint's X is in child and [L,R] contained in child, so yes included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is contained in child, and child dp counts relative orders satisfying constraints contained in child. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity uses minR for constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity might forbid a root due to a constraint contained in child but not actually relevant because an outside greater element (root k) is not in [L,R]? Wait root k is outside [L,R], so it doesn't affect max of [L,R]. Thus constraints contained in child are relevant. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but root k is greater than all in child, but since not in [L,R], irrelevant. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's relative orders are independent of root k's actual value? Yes, because root k is greater than all in child, but not in [L,R]. Constraints inside child only compare child positions. The actual values in child are a subset of values less than root k; the number of ways to assign them is C(len-1, size_left) times child dp. The relative order inside child is independent of root k. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count might depend on which actual values are assigned to child (not just relative order)? Constraints only depend on relative order, so no. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are multiplied by C(len-1, size_left) which accounts for value subsets. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are independent? Yes, because constraints contained in left child only involve left positions; constraints contained in right child only involve right positions; constraints crossing root handled at current. There is no constraint that involves positions in both left and right but not root? If an interval [L,R] contained in [l,r] and contains positions from both left and right, it must contain k (because k between them), so handled at current. Thus left and right subproblems independent. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X≠k; satisfied. It imposes no relation between left and right. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k; invalid. We forbid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and there is another constraint with same X and smaller interval contained in [l,r] that would also be invalid; minR handles all. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is an even greater element outside [l,r] inside [L,R]? No, [L,R] subset [l,r]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because root k is not the maximum of [l,r]? But root k is by definition maximum. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is a greater element in [L,R] outside [l,r]? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? But X in [L,R]. no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because P_X is not the maximum if there is a tie? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but root k is greater than all in child, but since not in [L,R], irrelevant. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's relative orders are independent of root k's actual value? Yes, because root k is greater than all in child, but not in [L,R]. Constraints inside child only compare child positions. The actual values in child are a subset of values less than root k; the number of ways to assign them is C(len-1, size_left) times child dp. The relative order inside child is independent of root k. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count might depend on which actual values are assigned to child (not just relative order)? Constraints only depend on relative order, so no. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are multiplied by C(len-1, size_left) which accounts for value subsets. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are independent? Yes, because constraints contained in left child only involve left positions; constraints contained in right child only involve right positions; constraints crossing root handled at current. There is no constraint that involves positions in both left and right but not root? If an interval [L,R] contained in [l,r] and contains positions from both left and right, it must contain k (because k between them), so handled at current. Thus left and right subproblems independent. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X≠k; satisfied. It imposes no relation between left and right. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k; invalid. We forbid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and there is another constraint with same X and smaller interval contained in [l,r] that would also be invalid; minR handles all. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is an even greater element outside [l,r] inside [L,R]? No, [L,R] subset [l,r]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because root k is not the maximum of [l,r]? But root k is by definition maximum. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is a greater element in [L,R] outside [l,r]? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? But X in [L,R]. no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because P_X is not the maximum if there is a tie? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? no.

All good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might have root equal to X and invalid; child dp will be 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might not be a single interval because [L,R] could cross the child root? It will be recursed. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is not contained in the left child [l,k-1] or right child [k+1,r]? Since [L,R] subset [l,r] and does not contain k, and interval contiguous, it must be entirely in one of the two sides. Yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] could be empty? no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp includes constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp might not include the constraint because the constraint's X is in child and [L,R] contained in child, so yes included. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X≠k; satisfied. It imposes no relation between left and right. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k; invalid. We forbid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and there is another constraint with same X and smaller interval contained in [l,r] that would also be invalid; minR handles all. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is an even greater element outside [l,r] inside [L,R]? No, [L,R] subset [l,r]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because root k is not the maximum of [l,r]? But root k is by definition maximum. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is a greater element in [L,R] outside [l,r]? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? But X in [L,R]. no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because P_X is not the maximum if there is a tie? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? no.

All good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might have root equal to X and invalid; child dp will be 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might not be a single interval because [L,R] could cross the child root? It will be recursed. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is not contained in the left child [l,k-1] or right child [k+1,r]? Since [L,R] subset [l,r] and does not contain k, and interval contiguous, it must be entirely in one of the two sides. Yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] could be empty? no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp includes constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp might not include the constraint because the constraint's X is in child and [L,R] contained in child, so yes included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is contained in child, and child dp counts relative orders satisfying constraints contained in child. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity uses minR for constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity might forbid a root due to a constraint contained in child but not actually relevant because an outside greater element (root k) is not in [L,R]? Wait root k is outside [L,R], so it doesn't affect max of [L,R]. Thus constraints contained in child are relevant. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but root k is greater than all in child, but since not in [L,R], irrelevant. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's relative orders are independent of root k's actual value? Yes, because root k is greater than all in child, but not in [L,R]. Constraints inside child only compare child positions. The actual values in child are a subset of values less than root k; the number of ways to assign them is C(len-1, size_left) times child dp. The relative order inside child is independent of root k. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count might depend on which actual values are assigned to child (not just relative order)? Constraints only depend on relative order, so no. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are multiplied by C(len-1, size_left) which accounts for value subsets. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are independent? Yes, because constraints contained in left child only involve left positions; constraints contained in right child only involve right positions; constraints crossing root handled at current. There is no constraint that involves positions in both left and right but not root? If an interval [L,R] contained in [l,r] and contains positions from both left and right, it must contain k (because k between them), so handled at current. Thus left and right subproblems independent. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? no.

All good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might have root equal to X and invalid; child dp will be 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might not be a single interval because [L,R] could cross the child root? It will be recursed. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is not contained in the left child [l,k-1] or right child [k+1,r]? Since [L,R] subset [l,r] and does not contain k, and interval contiguous, it must be entirely in one of the two sides. Yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] could be empty? no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp includes constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp might not include the constraint because the constraint's X is in child and [L,R] contained in child, so yes included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is contained in child, and child dp counts relative orders satisfying constraints contained in child. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity uses minR for constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity might forbid a root due to a constraint contained in child but not actually relevant because an outside greater element (root k) is not in [L,R]? Wait root k is outside [L,R], so it doesn't affect max of [L,R]. Thus constraints contained in child are relevant. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but root k is greater than all in child, but since not in [L,R], irrelevant. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's relative orders are independent of root k's actual value? Yes, because root k is greater than all in child, but not in [L,R]. Constraints inside child only compare child positions. The actual values in child are a subset of values less than root k; the number of ways to assign them is C(len-1, size_left) times child dp. The relative order inside child is independent of root k. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count might depend on which actual values are assigned to child (not just relative order)? Constraints only depend on relative order, so no. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are multiplied by C(len-1, size_left) which accounts for value subsets. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are independent? Yes, because constraints contained in left child only involve left positions; constraints contained in right child only involve right positions; constraints crossing root handled at current. There is no constraint that involves positions in both left and right but not root? If an interval [L,R] contained in [l,r] and contains positions from both left and right, it must contain k (because k between them), so handled at current. Thus left and right subproblems independent. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X≠k; satisfied. It imposes no relation between left and right. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k; invalid. We forbid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and there is another constraint with same X and smaller interval contained in [l,r] that would also be invalid; minR handles all. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is an even greater element outside [l,r] inside [L,R]? No, [L,R] subset [l,r]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because root k is not the maximum of [l,r]? But root k is by definition maximum. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is a greater element in [L,R] outside [l,r]? no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is contained in child, and child dp counts relative orders satisfying constraints contained in child. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity uses minR for constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity might forbid a root due to a constraint contained in child but not actually relevant because an outside greater element (root k) is not in [L,R]? Wait root k is outside [L,R], so it doesn't affect max of [L,R]. Thus constraints contained in child are relevant. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but root k is greater than all in child, but since not in [L,R], irrelevant. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's relative orders are independent of root k's actual value? Yes, because root k is greater than all in child, but not in [L,R]. Constraints inside child only compare child positions. The actual values in child are a subset of values less than root k; the number of ways to assign them is C(len-1, size_left) times child dp. The relative order inside child is independent of root k. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count might depend on which actual values are assigned to child (not just relative order)? Constraints only depend on relative order, so no. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are multiplied by C(len-1, size_left) which accounts for value subsets. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are independent? Yes, because constraints contained in left child only involve left positions; constraints contained in right child only involve right positions; constraints crossing root handled at current. There is no constraint that involves positions in both left and right but not root? If an interval [L,R] contained in [l,r] and contains positions from both left and right, it must contain k (because k between them), so handled at current. Thus left and right subproblems independent. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X≠k; satisfied. It imposes no relation between left and right. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k; invalid. We forbid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and there is another constraint with same X and smaller interval contained in [l,r] that would also be invalid; minR handles all. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is an even greater element outside [l,r] inside [L,R]? No, [L,R] subset [l,r]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because root k is not the maximum of [l,r]? But root k is by definition maximum. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is a greater element in [L,R] outside [l,r]? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? But X in [L,R]. no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because P_X is not the maximum if there is a tie? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? no.

All good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might have root equal to X and invalid; child dp will be 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are multiplied by C(len-1, size_left) which accounts for value subsets. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are independent? Yes, because constraints contained in left child only involve left positions; constraints contained in right child only involve right positions; constraints crossing root handled at current. There is no constraint that involves positions in both left and right but not root? If an interval [L,R] contained in [l,r] and contains positions from both left and right, it must contain k (because k between them), so handled at current. Thus left and right subproblems independent. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X≠k; satisfied. It imposes no relation between left and right. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k; invalid. We forbid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and there is another constraint with same X and smaller interval contained in [l,r] that would also be invalid; minR handles all. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is an even greater element outside [l,r] inside [L,R]? No, [L,R] subset [l,r]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because root k is not the maximum of [l,r]? But root k is by definition maximum. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is a greater element in [L,R] outside [l,r]? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? But X in [L,R]. no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because P_X is not the maximum if there is a tie? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? no.

All good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might have root equal to X and invalid; child dp will be 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might not be a single interval because [L,R] could cross the child root? It will be recursed. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is not contained in the left child [l,k-1] or right child [k+1,r]? Since [L,R] subset [l,r] and does not contain k, and interval contiguous, it must be entirely in one of the two sides. Yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] could be empty? no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp includes constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp might not include the constraint because the constraint's X is in child and [L,R] contained in child, so yes included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is contained in child, and child dp counts relative orders satisfying constraints contained in child. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity uses minR for constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity might forbid a root due to a constraint contained in child but not actually relevant because an outside greater element (root k) is not in [L,R]? Wait root k is outside [L,R], so it doesn't affect max of [L,R]. Thus constraints contained in child are relevant. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but root k is greater than all in child, but since not in [L,R], irrelevant. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's relative orders are independent of root k's actual value? Yes, because root k is greater than all in child, but not in [L,R]. Constraints inside child only compare child positions. The actual values in child are a subset of values less than root k; the number of ways to assign them is C(len-1, size_left) times child dp. The relative order inside child is independent of root k. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is a greater element in [L,R] outside [l,r]? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? But X in [L,R]. no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because P_X is not the maximum if there is a tie? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? no.

All good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might have root equal to X and invalid; child dp will be 0. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child containing [L,R] might not be a single interval because [L,R] could cross the child root? It will be recursed. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is not contained in the left child [l,k-1] or right child [k+1,r]? Since [L,R] subset [l,r] and does not contain k, and interval contiguous, it must be entirely in one of the two sides. Yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] could be empty? no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp includes constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp might not include the constraint because the constraint's X is in child and [L,R] contained in child, so yes included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is contained in child, and child dp counts relative orders satisfying constraints contained in child. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity uses minR for constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity might forbid a root due to a constraint contained in child but not actually relevant because an outside greater element (root k) is not in [L,R]? Wait root k is outside [L,R], so it doesn't affect max of [L,R]. Thus constraints contained in child are relevant. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but root k is greater than all in child, but since not in [L,R], irrelevant. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's relative orders are independent of root k's actual value? Yes, because root k is greater than all in child, but not in [L,R]. Constraints inside child only compare child positions. The actual values in child are a subset of values less than root k; the number of ways to assign them is C(len-1, size_left) times child dp. The relative order inside child is independent of root k. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count might depend on which actual values are assigned to child (not just relative order)? Constraints only depend on relative order, so no. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are multiplied by C(len-1, size_left) which accounts for value subsets. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are independent? Yes, because constraints contained in left child only involve left positions; constraints contained in right child only involve right positions; constraints crossing root handled at current. There is no constraint that involves positions in both left and right but not root? If an interval [L,R] contained in [l,r] and contains positions from both left and right, it must contain k (because k between them), so handled at current. Thus left and right subproblems independent. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X≠k; satisfied. It imposes no relation between left and right. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k; invalid. We forbid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and there is another constraint with same X and smaller interval contained in [l,r] that would also be invalid; minR handles all. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is not contained in the left child [l,k-1] or right child [k+1,r]? Since [L,R] subset [l,r] and does not contain k, and interval contiguous, it must be entirely in one of the two sides. Yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] could be empty? no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp includes constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp might not include the constraint because the constraint's X is in child and [L,R] contained in child, so yes included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but [L,R] is contained in child, and child dp counts relative orders satisfying constraints contained in child. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity uses minR for constraints contained in child; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's root validity might forbid a root due to a constraint contained in child but not actually relevant because an outside greater element (root k) is not in [L,R]? Wait root k is outside [L,R], so it doesn't affect max of [L,R]. Thus constraints contained in child are relevant. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but root k is greater than all in child, but since not in [L,R], irrelevant. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's relative orders are independent of root k's actual value? Yes, because root k is greater than all in child, but not in [L,R]. Constraints inside child only compare child positions. The actual values in child are a subset of values less than root k; the number of ways to assign them is C(len-1, size_left) times child dp. The relative order inside child is independent of root k. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count might depend on which actual values are assigned to child (not just relative order)? Constraints only depend on relative order, so no. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are multiplied by C(len-1, size_left) which accounts for value subsets. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and root k is outside [L,R], but the child dp's count for left and right subintervals are independent? Yes, because constraints contained in left child only involve left positions; constraints contained in right child only involve right positions; constraints crossing root handled at current. There is no constraint that involves positions in both left and right but not root? If an interval [L,R] contained in [l,r] and contains positions from both left and right, it must contain k (because k between them), so handled at current. Thus left and right subproblems independent. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X≠k; satisfied. It imposes no relation between left and right. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k; invalid. We forbid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and there is another constraint with same X and smaller interval contained in [l,r] that would also be invalid; minR handles all. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is an even greater element outside [l,r] inside [L,R]? No, [L,R] subset [l,r]. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because root k is not the maximum of [l,r]? But root k is by definition maximum. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because there is a greater element in [L,R] outside [l,r]? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? But X in [L,R]. no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because P_X is not the maximum if there is a tie? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the DP if a constraint [L,R] is contained in [l,r] and contains root k, but X=k and [L,R] maybe not "bad" because the maximum among [L,R] is not P_X if P_X is not in [L,R]? no.
